### 55 Victimisation.

[^F386]1 [^F386]or the purposes of Part II or Part III [^F386][[^F387](other than sections 24A to 24L)][^F386], a person (“A”) discriminates against another person (“B”) if—

[^F386]a [^F386]he treats B less favourably than he treats or would treat other persons whose circumstances are the same as B’s; and

[^F386]b [^F386]he does so for a reason mentioned in subsection (2).

[^F386]2 [^F386]The reasons are that—

[^F386]a [^F386]B has—

[^F386]i [^F386]brought proceedings against A or any other person under this Act [^F386][[^F388]or Part III of the 2005 Order][^F386] ; or

[^F386]ii [^F386]given evidence or information in connection with such proceedings brought by any person; or

[^F386]iii [^F386]otherwise done anything under [^F386][[^F389], or by reference to,][^F386] this Act [^F386][[^F388]or Part III of the 2005 Order][^F386] in relation to A or any other person; or

[^F386]iv [^F386]alleged that A or any other person has (whether or not the allegation so states) contravened this Act [^F386][[^F388]or Part III of the 2005 Order][^F386] ; or

[^F386]b [^F386]A believes or suspects that B has done or intends to do any of those things.

[^F386]3 [^F386]Where B is a disabled person, or a person who has had a disability, the disability in question shall be disregarded in comparing his circumstances with those of any other person for the purposes of subsection (1)(a).

[^F386][[^F390]3A [^F386]For the purposes of Chapter I of Part III of the 2005 Order—

[^F386]a [^F386]references in subsection (2) to B include references to—

[^F386]i [^F386]a person who is, for the purposes of that Chapter, B's parent; and

[^F386]ii [^F386]a sibling of B; and

[^F386]b [^F386]references in that subsection to this Act or Part III of the 2005 Order are, as respects a person mentioned in sub-paragraph (i) or (ii) of paragraph (a), to be read as references to that Chapter.]

[^F386]4 [^F386]Subsection (1) does not apply to treatment of a person because of an allegation made by him if the allegation was false and not made in good faith.

[^F386][[^F391]5 [^F386]In the case of an act which constitutes discrimination by virtue of this section, sections 4, 4B, 4D, 4G, 6A, 7A, 13, 14A, 14C [^F386][[^F392], 15B][^F386] and 16A also apply to discrimination against a person who is not disabled.

[^F386]6 [^F386][^F393]. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .]

### 56 [[^F394] Help for aggrieved persons in obtaining information etc.

[^F395]1 [^F395]For the purposes of this section—

[^F395]a [^F395]a person who considers that he may have been—

[^F395]i [^F395]discriminated against in contravention of Part II or III, or

[^F395]ii [^F395]subjected to harassment in contravention of Part II or section 21A(2),

[^F395]is referred to as “[^F395]the person aggrieved[^F395]”; and

[^F395]b [^F395]a person against whom the person aggrieved may decide to institute, or has instituted, proceedings in respect of such discrimination or harassment is referred to as “[^F395]the respondent[^F395]”.

[^F395]2 [^F395]With a view to helping the person aggrieved decide whether to institute proceedings and, if he does so, to formulate and present his case in the most effective manner, the Office shall by order prescribe—

[^F395]a [^F395]forms by which the person aggrieved may question the respondent on his reasons for doing any relevant act, or on any other matter which is or may be relevant; and

[^F395]b [^F395]forms by which the respondent may if he so wishes reply to any questions.

[^F395]3 [^F395]Where the person aggrieved questions the respondent in accordance with forms prescribed by an order under subsection (2)—

[^F395]a [^F395]the question, and any reply by the respondent (whether in accordance with such an order or not), shall be admissible as evidence in any proceedings under Part II or III;

[^F395]b [^F395]if it appears to the court or tribunal in any such proceedings—

[^F395]i [^F395]that the respondent deliberately, and without reasonable excuse, omitted to reply within the period of eight weeks beginning with the day on which the question was served on him, or

[^F395]ii [^F395]that the respondent's reply is evasive or equivocal,

[^F395]it may draw any inference which it considers it just and equitable to draw, including an inference that the respondent committed an unlawful act.

[^F395]4 [^F395]The Office may by order—

[^F395]a [^F395]prescribe the period within which questions must be duly served in order to be admissible under subsection (3)(a); and

[^F395]b [^F395]prescribe the manner in which a question, and any reply by the respondent, may be duly served.

[^F395]5 [^F395]County court rules may enable a court entertaining a claim under section 25 to determine, before the date fixed for the hearing of the claim, whether a question or reply is admissible under this section or not.

[^F395]6 [^F395]In proceedings in respect of a section 21B claim, subsection (3)(b) does not apply in relation to a failure to reply, or a particular reply, if the following conditions are met—

[^F395]a [^F395]that at the time of doing any relevant act, the respondent was carrying out public investigator functions or was a public prosecutor; and

[^F395]b [^F395]that the respondent reasonably believes that a reply or (as the case may be) a different reply would be likely to prejudice any criminal investigation, any decision to institute criminal proceedings or any criminal proceedings or would reveal the reasons behind a decision not to institute, or a decision not to continue, criminal proceedings.

[^F395]7 [^F395]The Office may by regulations provide for this section not to have effect, or to have effect with prescribed modifications, in relation to section 21B claims of a prescribed description.

[^F395]8 [^F395]This section is without prejudice to any other enactment or rule of law regulating interlocutory and preliminary matters in proceedings before a county court or industrial tribunal, and has effect subject to any enactment or rule of law regulating the admissibility of evidence in such proceedings.

[^F395]9 [^F395]In this section “[^F395]section 21B claim[^F395]” means a claim under section 25 by virtue of section 21B.]

### 57 Aiding unlawful acts.

[^F396]1 [^F396]A person who knowingly aids another person to do an [^F396][[^F397]unlawful act][^F396][[^F397]act made unlawful by this Act][^F396] is to be treated for the purposes of this Act as himself doing the same kind of unlawful act.

[^F396]2 [^F396]For the purposes of subsection (1), an employee or agent for whose act the employer or principal is liable under section 58 (or would be so liable but for section 58(5)) shall be taken to have aided the employer or principal to do the act.

[^F396]3 [^F396]For the purposes of this section, a person does not knowingly aid another to do an unlawful act if—

[^F396]a [^F396]he acts in reliance on a statement made to him by that other person that, because of any provision of this Act, the act would not be unlawful; and

[^F396]b [^F396]it is reasonable for him to rely on the statement.

[^F396]4 [^F396]A person who knowingly or recklessly makes such a statement which is false or misleading in a material respect is guilty of an offence.

[^F396]5 [^F396]Any person guilty of an offence under subsection (4) shall be liable on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding level 5 on the standard scale.

[^F396][[^F398]6 [^F396]“[^F396]Unlawful act[^F396]” means an act made unlawful by any provision of this Act other than a provision contained in Chapter 1 of Part 4.]

### 58 Liability of employers and principals.

[^F399]1 [^F399]Anything done by a person in the course of his employment shall be treated for the purposes of this Act as also done by his employer, whether or not it was done with the employer’s knowledge or approval.

[^F399]2 [^F399]Anything done by a person as agent for another person with the authority of that other person shall be treated for the purposes of this Act as also done by that other person.

[^F399]3 [^F399]Subsection (2) applies whether the authority was—

[^F399]a [^F399]express or implied; or

[^F399]b [^F399]given before or after the act in question was done.

[^F399]4 [^F399]Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply in relation to an offence under section 57(4).

[^F399]5 [^F399]In proceedings under this Act against any person in respect of an act alleged to have been done by an employee of his, it shall be a defence for that person to prove that he took such steps as were reasonably practicable to prevent the employee from—

[^F399]a [^F399]doing that act; or

[^F399]b [^F399]doing, in the course of his employment, acts of that description.

### 59 Statutory authority and national security etc.

[^F400]1 [^F400]Nothing in this Act makes unlawful any act done—

[^F400]a [^F400]in pursuance of any enactment; or

[^F400][[^F401]b [^F400]in pursuance of any instrument made under any enactment by—

[^F400]i [^F400]a Minister of the Crown,

[^F400]ii [^F400]a member of the Scottish Executive, [^F400][^F402]. . .

[^F400]iii [^F400]the National Assembly for Wales [^F400][[^F403]constituted by the Government of Wales Act 1998, or

[^F400]iv [^F400]the Welsh Ministers, the First Minister for Wales or the Counsel General to the Welsh Assembly Government.]

[^F400]or

[^F400]c [^F400]to comply with any condition or requirement—

[^F400]i [^F400]imposed by a Minister of the Crown (whether before or after the passing of this Act) by virtue of any enactment,

[^F400]ii [^F400]imposed by a member of the Scottish Executive (whether before or after the coming into force of this sub-paragraph) by virtue of any enactment, [^F400][^F404]. . .

[^F400]iii [^F400]imposed by the National Assembly for Wales [^F400][[^F405]constituted by the Government of Wales Act 1998][^F400](whether before or after the coming into force of this sub-paragraph) by virtue of any enactment[^F400][[^F406] or

[^F400]iv [^F400]imposed by the Welsh Ministers, the First Minister for Wales or the Counsel General to the Welsh Assembly Government.]]

[^F400]2 [^F400]In subsection (1) “[^F400]enactment[^F400]” includes one passed or made after the date on which this Act is passed and “[^F400]instrument[^F400]” includes one made after that date.

[^F400][[^F407]2A [^F400]Nothing in –

[^F400]a [^F400]Part II, or

[^F400]b [^F400]Part III to the extent that it relates to the provision of employment services,

[^F400]makes unlawful any act done for the purpose of safeguarding national security if the doing of the act was justified by that purpose.]

[^F400][[^F408]2A [^F400]Nothing in—

[^F400]a [^F400]Part 2 of this Act, or

[^F400]b [^F400]Part 3 of this Act to the extent that it relates to the provision of employment services,

[^F400]makes unlawful any act done for the purpose of safeguarding national security if the doing of the act was justified by that purpose.]

[^F400]3 [^F400]Nothing in [^F400][[^F409]any other provision of][^F400] this Act makes unlawful any act done for the purpose of safeguarding national security.

### 59A [[^F410]National security

[^F411]1 [^F411]Rules of court may make provision for enabling a county court or sheriff court in which a claim is brought in respect of alleged discrimination contrary to this Act (including anything treated by virtue of this Act as amounting to discrimination contrary to this Act), where the court considers it expedient in the interests of national security—

[^F411]a [^F411]to exclude from all or part of the proceedings—

[^F411]i [^F411]the claimant;

[^F411]ii [^F411]the claimant's representatives;

[^F411]iii [^F411]any assessors;

[^F411]b [^F411]to permit a claimant or representative who has been excluded to make a statement to the court before the commencement of the proceedings, or the part of the proceedings, from which he is excluded;

[^F411]c [^F411]to take steps to keep secret all or part of the reasons for the court's decision in the proceedings.

[^F411]2 [^F411]The Attorney General or, in Scotland, the Advocate General for Scotland, may appoint a person to represent the interests of a claimant in, or in any part of, proceedings from which the claimant or his representatives are excluded by virtue of subsection (1).

[^F411]3 [^F411]A person may be appointed under subsection (2) only—

[^F411]a [^F411]in relation to proceedings in England and Wales, if he has a general qualification (within the meaning of section 71 of the Courts and Legal Services Act 1990 (c. 41)), or

[^F411]b [^F411]in relation to proceedings in Scotland, if he is—

[^F411]i [^F411]an advocate, or

[^F411]ii [^F411]qualified to practice as a solicitor in Scotland.

[^F411]4 [^F411]A person appointed under subsection (2) shall not be responsible to the person whose interests he is appointed to represent.]

## Part VIII — Miscellaneous

### 60 Appointment by Secretary of State of advisers.

[^F412]1 [^F412]The Secretary of State may appoint such persons as he thinks fit to advise or assist him in connection with matters relating to the employment of disabled persons and persons who have had a disability.

[^F412]2 [^F412]Persons may be appointed by the Secretary of State to act generally or in relation to a particular area or locality.

[^F412]3 [^F412]The Secretary of State may pay to any person appointed under this section such allowances and compensation for loss of earnings as he considers appropriate.

[^F412]4 [^F412]The approval of the Treasury is required for any payment under this section.

[^F412]5 [^F412]In subsection (1) “[^F412]employment[^F412]” includes self-employment.

[^F412]6 [^F412]The Secretary of State may by order—

[^F412]a [^F412]provide for section 17 of, and Schedule 2 to, the [^F412][^M4]Disabled Persons (Employment) Act 1944 (national advisory council and district advisory committees) to cease to have effect—

[^F412]i [^F412]so far as concerns the national advisory council; or

[^F412]ii [^F412]so far as concerns district advisory committees; or

[^F412]b [^F412]repeal that section and Schedule.

[^F412]7 [^F412]At any time before the coming into force of an order under paragraph (b) of subsection (6), section 17 of the Act of 1944 shall have effect as if in subsection (1), after “disabled persons” in each case there were inserted “, and persons who have had a disability,” and as if at the end of the section there were added—

> [^F412]3 [^F412]For the purposes of this section—
>
> [^F412]a [^F412]a person is a disabled person if he is a disabled person for the purposes of the [^F412][^M5]Disability Discrimination Act 1995; and
>
> [^F412]b [^F412]“[^F412]disability[^F412]” has the same meaning as in that Act.

[^F412]8 [^F412]At any time before the coming into force of an order under paragraph (a)(i) or (b) of subsection (6), section 16 of the [^F412][^M6]Chronically Sick and Disabled Persons Act 1970 (which extends the functions of the national advisory council) shall have effect as if after “disabled persons” in each case there were inserted [^F412]“ , and persons who have had a disability, ”[^F412] and as if at the end of the section there were added—

> [^F412]2 [^F412]For the purposes of this section—
>
> [^F412]a [^F412]a person is a disabled person if he is a disabled person for the purposes of the Disability Discrimination Act 1995; and
>
> [^F412]b [^F412]“[^F412]disability[^F412]” has the same meaning as in that Act.

### 61 Amendment of Disabled Persons (Employment) Act 1944.

[^F413]1 [^F413]Section 15 of the [^F413][^M7]Disabled Persons (Employment) Act 1944 (which gives the Secretary of State power to make arrangements for the provision of supported employment) is amended as set out in subsections (2) to (5).

[^F413]2 [^F413]In subsection (1)—

[^F413]a [^F413]for “persons registered as handicapped by disablement” substitute [^F413]“ disabled persons ”[^F413];

[^F413]b [^F413]for “their disablement” substitute [^F413]“ their disability ”[^F413]; and

[^F413]c [^F413]for “are not subject to disablement” substitute [^F413]“ do not have a disability ”[^F413].

[^F413]3 [^F413]In subsection (2), for the words from “any of one or more companies” to “so required and prohibited” substitute [^F413]“ any company, association or body ”[^F413].

[^F413]4 [^F413]After subsection (2) insert—

> [^F413]2A [^F413]The only kind of company which the Minister himself may form in exercising his powers under this section is a company which is—
>
> [^F413]a [^F413]required by its constitution to apply its profits, if any, or other income in promoting its objects; and
>
> [^F413]b [^F413]prohibited by its constitution from paying any dividend to its members.

[^F413]5 [^F413]After subsection (5) insert—

> [^F413]5A [^F413]For the purposes of this section—
>
> [^F413]a [^F413]a person is a disabled person if he is a disabled person for the purposes of the Disability Discrimination Act 1995; and
>
> [^F413]b [^F413]“[^F413]disability[^F413]” has the same meaning as in that Act.

[^F413]6 [^F413]The provisions of section 16 (preference to be given under section 15 of that Act to ex-service men and women) shall become subsection (1) of that section and at the end insert—

> [^F413]and whose disability is due to that service.
>
> [^F413]2 [^F413]For the purposes of subsection (1) of this section, a disabled person’s disability shall be treated as due to service of a particular kind only in such circumstances as may be prescribed.

[^F413]7 [^F413]The following provisions of the Act of 1944 shall cease to have effect—

[^F413]a [^F413]section 1 (definition of “disabled person”);

[^F413]b [^F413]sections 6 to 8 (the register of disabled persons);

[^F413]c [^F413]sections 9 to 11 (obligations on employers with substantial staffs to employ a quota of registered persons);

[^F413]d [^F413]section 12 (the designated employment scheme for persons registered as handicapped by disablement);

[^F413]e [^F413]section 13 (interpretation of provisions repealed by this Act);

[^F413]f [^F413]section 14 (records to be kept by employers);

[^F413]g [^F413]section 19 (proceedings in relation to offences); and

[^F413]h [^F413]section 21 (application as respects place of employment, and nationality).

[^F413]8 [^F413]Any provision of subordinate legislation in which “[^F413]disabled person[^F413]” is defined by reference to the Act of 1944 shall be construed as if that expression had the same meaning as in this Act.

[^F413]9 [^F413]Subsection (8) does not prevent the further amendment of any such provision by subordinate legislation.

### 63 Restriction of publicity: Employment Appeal Tribunal.

### 64 Application to Crown etc.

[^F416][[^F417]A1 [^F416]The following provisions bind the Crown—

[^F416]a [^F416]sections 21B to 21E and Part 5A, and

[^F416]b [^F416]the other provisions of this Act so far as applying for the purposes of provisions mentioned in paragraph (a);

[^F416]and sections 57 and 58 shall apply for purposes of provisions mentioned in paragraph (a) as if service as a Crown servant were employment by the Crown.]

[^F416]as it applies to employment by a private person.

### 64A [[^F432]Police

[^F433]. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .]

### 64B [[^F434]Other police bodies

(1) For the purposes of Part II, the holding of the office of constable otherwise than as a police officer shall be treated as employment –

(a) by the chief officer of police as respects any act done by him in relation to that office or a holder of it;

(b) by the police authority as respects any act done by it in relation to that office or the holder of it;

(2) For the purposes of section 58 –

(a) the holding of the office of constable otherwise than as a police officer shall be treated as employment by the chief officer of police (and as not being employment by any other person); and

(b) anything done by a person holding such an office in the performance, or purported performance, of his functions shall be treated as done in the course of that employment.

(3) There shall be paid out of the police fund –

(a) any compensation, damages or costs awarded against a chief officer of police in any proceedings brought against him under Part II [[^F435]or III], and any costs incurred by him in any such proceedings so far as not recovered by him in the proceedings; and

(b) any sum required by a chief officer of police for the settlement of any claim made against him under Part II if the settlement is approved by the police authority.

(4) A police authority may, in such cases and to such extent as appear to it to be appropriate, pay out of the police fund –

(a) any compensation, damages or costs awarded in proceedings under Part II [[^F435]or III] against a person under the direction and control of the chief officer of police;

(b) any costs incurred and not recovered by such a person in such proceedings; and

(c) any sum required in connection with the settlement of a claim that has or might have given rise to such proceedings.

(5) A police authority may make arrangements for the legal representation of any person under the direction and control of the chief officer of police in any proceedings mentioned in subsection (4).

[[^F436](6) Subject to subsection (8), in this section in relation to any body of constables—

(a) “chief officer of police” means the person who has the direction and control of the body;

(b) “police authority” means the authority by which the members of the body are paid; and

(c) “police fund” means money provided by that authority.]

(7) Nothing in subsections (3) to (6) applies in relation to the police.

(8) In relation to a constable of a force who is not under the direction and control of the chief officer of police for that force, references in this section to the chief officer of police are references to the chief officer of the force under whose direction and control he is, and references in this section to the police authority are references to the relevant police authority for that force.]

### 67 Regulations and orders.

[^F439]1 [^F439]Any power under this Act to make regulations or orders shall be exercisable by statutory rule for the purposes of the Statutory Rules (Northern Ireland) Order 1979.

[^F439]2 [^F439]Any such power may be exercised to make different provision for different cases, including different provision for different areas or localities.

[^F439]3 [^F439]Any such power, includes power—

[^F439]a [^F439]to make such incidental, supplementary, consequential or transitional provision as appears to the Northern Ireland department exercising the power to be expedient; and

[^F439]b [^F439]to provide for a person to exercise a discretion in dealing with any matter.

[^F439][[^F440]3A [^F439]Where regulations under section 21D(7)(b) provide for the omission of section 21D(5), the provision that may be made by the regulations in exercise of the power conferred by subsection (3)(a) includes provision amending section 21D for the purpose of omitting references to section 21D(5).

[^F439]3B [^F439]The provision that may be made by regulations under section 21G(5)(b) in exercise of the power conferred by subsection (3)(a) includes provision amending or repealing section 21G(4).]

[^F439][[^F441]4 [^F439]Subsection (4A) applies to—

[^F439]a [^F439]the first regulations to be made under section 21H(1);

[^F439]b [^F439]regulations under section 47J(3);

[^F439]c [^F439]regulations under section 67A(3);

[^F439]d [^F439]regulations under paragraph 6A(2) of Schedule 1.

[^F439]4A [^F439]No regulations to which this subsection applies shall be made unless a draft of the regulations has been laid before, and approved by a resolution of, the Assembly.

[^F439]4B [^F439]An order under section 47(1), if made without a draft having been laid before and approved by a resolution of, the Assembly shall be subject to annulment in pursuance of a resolution of the Assembly; but the exercise of the discretion conferred by this subsection is subject to section 67A.]

[^F439]5 [^F439]Any other order made under this Act, other than an order under section 3(9), [^F439][[^F442]54A(6)(a)][^F439] or 70(3), and any regulations made under this Act shall be subject to negative resolution within the meaning of section 41(6) of the Interpretation Act (Northern Ireland) 1954 as if they were statutory instruments within the meaning of that Act.

[^F439]6 [^F439]Section 41(3) of the Interpretation Act (Northern Ireland) 1954 shall apply in relation to any instrument or document which by virtue of this Act is required to be laid before the Assembly as if it were a statutory instrument or statutory document within the meaning of that Act.

[^F439]7 [^F439]Subsection (1) does not require an order under section 43 which applies only to a specified vehicle, or to vehicles of a specified person, to be made by statutory rule.

[^F439]8 [^F439]Nothing in section 40(6) or 46(5) affects the powers conferred by subsections (2) and (3).

[^F447][[^F464]2 [^F447]Employment (including employment on board a ship to which subsection (2B) applies or on an aircraft or hovercraft to which subsection (2C) applies) is to be regarded as being employment at an establishment in Northern Ireland if the employee –

[^F447]a [^F447]does his work wholly or partly in Northern Ireland; or,

[^F447]b [^F447]does his work wholly outside Northern Ireland and subsection (2A) applies.

[^F447]2A [^F447]This subsection applies if –

[^F447]a [^F447]the employer has a place of business at an establishment in Northern Ireland;

[^F447]b [^F447]the work is for the purposes of the business carried on at the establishment; and

[^F447]c [^F447]the employee is ordinarily resident in Northern Ireland –

[^F447]i [^F447]at the time when he applies for or is offered the employment, or

[^F447]ii [^F447]at any time during the course of the employment.

[^F447]2B [^F447]This subsection applies to a ship if –

[^F447]a [^F447]it is registered at a port of registry in Northern Ireland; or

[^F447]b [^F447]it belongs to or is possessed by Her Majesty in right of the Government of the United Kingdom.

[^F447]2C [^F447]This subsection applies to an aircraft or hovercraft if –

[^F447]a [^F447]it is –

[^F447]i [^F447]registered in the United Kingdom, and

[^F447]ii [^F447]operated by a person who has his principal place of business, or is ordinarily resident, in Northern Ireland; or

[^F447]b [^F447]it belongs to or is possessed by Her Majesty in right of the Government of the United Kingdom.

[^F447]2D [^F447]The following are not to be regarded as being employment at an establishment in Northern Ireland –

[^F447]a [^F447]employment on board a ship to which subsection (2B) does not apply;

[^F447]b [^F447]employment on an aircraft or hovercraft to which subsection (2C) does not apply.]

[^F447]4 [^F447]Employment of a prescribed kind, or in prescribed circumstances, is to be regarded as not being employment at an establishment in [^F447][[^F465]Northern Ireland][^F447].

### 70 Short title, commencement, extent etc.

[^F468]1 [^F468]This Act may be cited as the Disability Discrimination Act 1995.

[^F468]2 [^F468]This section (apart from subsections (4), (5) and (7)) comes into force on the passing of this Act.

[^F468][[^F469]2A [^F468]The following provisions of this Act—

[^F468]b [^F468]section 36A, and

[^F468]come into force 2 months after the passing of the Local Transport Act 2008.]

[^F468]3 [^F468]The other provisions of this Act come into force on such day as the Secretary of State may by order appoint and different days may be appointed for different purposes.

[^F468]4 [^F468]Schedule 6 makes consequential amendments.

[^F468]5 [^F468]The repeals set out in Schedule 7 shall have effect.

[^F468][[^F470]5A [^F468]Sections 7A [^F468][[^F471]7B and 49G][^F468] extend to England and Wales only.

[^F468]5B [^F468]Sections 7C and 7D extend to Scotland only.]

[^F468]6 [^F468][[^F472]Subject to subsections (5A) and (5B), this Act extends to England and Wales, Scotland and Northern Ireland;][^F468] but in their application to Northern Ireland the provisions of this Act mentioned in Schedule 8 shall have effect subject to the modifications set out in that Schedule.

## SCHEDULE 1 — Provisions Supplementing Section 1

*Section 1(1).*

### Impairment

**1** [^F475]1 [^F475][^F476]. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .

[^F475]2 [^F475]Regulations may make provision, for the purposes of this Act—

[^F475]a [^F475]for conditions of a prescribed description to be treated as amounting to impairments;

[^F475]b [^F475]for conditions of a prescribed description to be treated as not amounting to impairments.

[^F475]3 [^F475]Regulations made under sub-paragraph (2) may make provision as to the meaning of “[^F475]condition[^F475]” for the purposes of those regulations.

### Long-term effects

**2** [^F477]1 [^F477]The effect of an impairment is a long-term effect if—

[^F477]a [^F477]it has lasted at least 12 months;

[^F477]b [^F477]the period for which it lasts is likely to be at least 12 months; or

[^F477]c [^F477]it is likely to last for the rest of the life of the person affected.

[^F477]2 [^F477]Where an impairment ceases to have a substantial adverse effect on a person’s ability to carry out normal day-to-day activities, it is to be treated as continuing to have that effect if that effect is likely to recur.

[^F477]3 [^F477]For the purposes of sub-paragraph (2), the likelihood of an effect recurring shall be disregarded in prescribed circumstances.

[^F477]4 [^F477]Regulations may prescribe circumstances in which, for the purposes of this Act—

[^F477]a [^F477]an effect which would not otherwise be a long-term effect is to be treated as such an effect; or

[^F477]b [^F477]an effect which would otherwise be a long-term effect is to be treated as not being such an effect.

### Severe disfigurement

**3** [^F478]1 [^F478]An impairment which consists of a severe disfigurement is to be treated as having a substantial adverse effect on the ability of the person concerned to carry out normal day-to-day activities.

[^F478]2 [^F478]Regulations may provide that in prescribed circumstances a severe disfigurement is not to be treated as having that effect.

[^F478]3 [^F478]Regulations under sub-paragraph (2) may, in particular, make provision with respect to deliberately acquired disfigurements.

### Normal day-to-day activities

**4** [^F479]1 [^F479]An impairment is to be taken to affect the ability of the person concerned to carry out normal day-to-day activities only if it affects one of the following—

[^F479]a [^F479]mobility;

[^F479]b [^F479]manual dexterity;

[^F479]c [^F479]physical co-ordination;

[^F479]d [^F479]continence;

[^F479]e [^F479]ability to lift, carry or otherwise move everyday objects;

[^F479]f [^F479]speech, hearing or eyesight;

[^F479]g [^F479]memory or ability to concentrate, learn or understand; or

[^F479]h [^F479]perception of the risk of physical danger.

[^F479][[^F480]i [^F479]taking part in normal social interaction; or

[^F479]j [^F479]forming social relationships]

[^F479]2 [^F479]Regulations may prescribe—

[^F479]a [^F479]circumstances in which an impairment which does not have an effect falling within sub-paragraph (1) is to be taken to affect the ability of the person concerned to carry out normal day-to-day activities;

[^F479]b [^F479]circumstances in which an impairment which has an effect falling within sub-paragraph (1) is to be taken not to affect the ability of the person concerned to carry out normal day-to-day activities.

### Substantial adverse effects

**5** [^F481]Regulations may make provision for the purposes of this Act—

[^F481]a [^F481]for an effect of a prescribed kind on the ability of a person to carry out normal day-to-day activities to be treated as a substantial adverse effect;

[^F481]b [^F481]for an effect of a prescribed kind on the ability of a person to carry out normal day-to-day activities to be treated as not being a substantial adverse effect.

### Effect of medical treatment

**6** [^F482]1 [^F482]An impairment which would be likely to have a substantial adverse effect on the ability of the person concerned to carry out normal day-to-day activities, but for the fact that measures are being taken to treat or correct it, is to be treated as having that effect.

[^F482]2 [^F482]In sub-paragraph (1) “[^F482]measures[^F482]” includes, in particular, medical treatment and the use of a prosthesis or other aid.

[^F482]3 [^F482]Sub-paragraph (1) does not apply—

[^F482]a [^F482]in relation to the impairment of a person’s sight, to the extent that the impairment is, in his case, correctable by spectacles or contact lenses or in such other ways as may be prescribed; or

[^F482]b [^F482]in relation to such other impairments as may be prescribed, in such circumstances as may be prescribed.

### Persons deemed to be disabled

**[[^F483]6A** [^F484]1 [^F484]Subject to sub-paragraph (2), a person who has cancer, HIV infection or multiple sclerosis is to be deemed to have a disability, and hence to be a disabled person.

[^F484]2 [^F484]Regulations may provide for sub-paragraph (1) not to apply in the case of a person who has cancer if he has cancer of a prescribed description.

[^F484]3 [^F484]A description of cancer prescribed under sub-paragraph (2) may (in particular) be framed by reference to consequences for a person of his having it.]

**7** [^F485]1 [^F485]Sub-paragraph (2) applies to any person whose name is, both on 12th January 1995 and on the date when this paragraph comes into force, in the register of disabled persons maintained under section 6 of the [^F485][^M12]Disabled Persons (Employment) Act 1944.

[^F485]2 [^F485]That person is to be deemed—

[^F485]a [^F485]during the initial period, to have a disability, and hence to be a disabled person; and

[^F485]b [^F485]afterwards, to have had a disability and hence to have been a disabled person during that period.

[^F485]3 [^F485]A certificate of registration shall be conclusive evidence, in relation to the person with respect to whom it was issued, of the matters certified.

[^F485]4 [^F485]Unless the contrary is shown, any document purporting to be a certificate of registration shall be taken to be such a certificate and to have been validly issued.

[^F485]5 [^F485]Regulations may provide for prescribed descriptions of person to be deemed to have disabilities, and hence to be disabled persons, for the purposes of this Act.

[^F485][[^F486]5A [^F485]The generality of sub-paragraph (5) shall not be taken to be prejudiced by the other provisions of this Schedule.]

[^F485]6 [^F485]Regulations may prescribe circumstances in which a person who has been deemed to be a disabled person by the provisions of sub-paragraph (1) or regulations made under sub-paragraph (5) is to be treated as no longer being deemed to be such a person.

[^F485]7 [^F485]In this paragraph—

- [^F485]“[^F485]certificate of registration[^F485]” means a certificate issued under regulations made under section 6 of the Act of 1944; and
- [^F485]“[^F485]initial period[^F485]” means the period of three years beginning with the date on which this paragraph comes into force.

### Progressive conditions

**8** [^F487]1 [^F487]Where—

[^F487]a [^F487]a person has a progressive condition (such as cancer, multiple sclerosis or muscular dystrophy or [^F487][[^F488]HIV infection][^F487]),

[^F487]b [^F487]as a result of that condition, he has an impairment which has (or had) an effect on his ability to carry out normal day-to-day activities, but

[^F487]c [^F487]that effect is not (or was not) a substantial adverse effect,

[^F487]he shall be taken to have an impairment which has such a substantial adverse effect if the condition is likely to result in his having such an impairment.

[^F487]2 [^F487]Regulations may make provision, for the purposes of this paragraph—

[^F487]a [^F487]for conditions of a prescribed description to be treated as being progressive;

[^F487]b [^F487]for conditions of a prescribed description to be treated as not being progressive.

### [[^F489]Interpretation

**9** [^F490]In this Schedule, “HIV infection” means infection by a virus capable of causing the Acquired Immune Deficiency Syndrome.]

## SCHEDULE 2 — Past Disabilities

#### Restriction on proceedings for breach of Part II

**2** [^F532][[^F533]1 [^F532]Except as provided by Part 2, no civil or criminal proceedings may be brought against any person in respect of an act merely because the act is unlawful under that Part.]

[^F532]2 [^F532]Sub-paragraph (1) does not prevent the making of an application for judicial review [^F532][[^F534] or the investigation or determination of any matter in accordance with Part 10 (investigations) of the Pension Schemes Act 1993 [^F532][^F535] by the Pensions Ombudsman”] [^F532][[^F536] or the investigation or determination of any matter in accordance with the Part X (investigations: the Pensions Ombudsman) of the Pension Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993 [^F532][^F537] by the Pensions Ombudsman][^F532].

[^F532][[^F538]3 [^F532]Sub-paragraph (1) does not prevent the bringing of proceedings in respect of an offence under section 16B(2B).]

#### Period within which proceedings must be brought

**3** [^F539]1 [^F539]An [^F539][[^F540]employment tribunal][^F539] shall not consider a complaint under [^F539][[^F541]section 17A or 25(8)][^F539] unless it is presented before the end of the period of three months beginning when the act complained of was done.

[^F539][[^F542]1A [^F539]Article 249B of the Employment Rights (Northern Ireland) Order 1996 (extension of time limits to facilitate conciliation before institution of proceedings) applies for the purposes of sub-paragraph (1)(a).

[^F539]1B [^F539]Sub-paragraphs (1) and (1A) shall be treated as provisions of the Employment Rights (Northern Ireland) Order 1996 for the purposes of Article 249B of that Order.]

[^F539]2 [^F539]A tribunal may consider any such complaint which is out of time if, in all the circumstances of the case, it considers that it is just and equitable to do so.

[^F539]3 [^F539]For the purposes of sub-paragraph (1)—

[^F539]a [^F539]where an unlawful act [^F543][^F539]. . . is attributable to a term in a contract, that act is to be treated as extending throughout the duration of the contract;

[^F539]b [^F539]any act extending over a period shall be treated as done at the end of that period; and

[^F539]c [^F539]a deliberate omission shall be treated as done when the person in question decided upon it.

[^F539]4 [^F539]In the absence of evidence establishing the contrary, a person shall be taken for the purposes of this paragraph to decide upon an omission—

[^F539]a [^F539]when he does an act inconsistent with doing the omitted act; or

[^F539]b [^F539]if he has done no such inconsistent act, when the period expires within which he might reasonably have been expected to do the omitted act if it was to be done.

#### Evidence

**4** [^F544]1 [^F544]In any proceedings under [^F544][[^F545]section 17A or 25(8)][^F544], a certificate signed by or on behalf of a Minister of the Crown and certifying—

[^F544]a [^F544]that any conditions or requirements specified in the certificate were imposed by a Minister of the Crown and were in operation at a time or throughout a time so specified, [^F544][^F546]. . .

[^F544][^F546]b [^F544]. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .

[^F544]shall be conclusive evidence of the matters certified.

[^F544][[^F547]1A [^F544]In any proceedings under section 17A or 25(8), a certificate signed by or on behalf of the Scottish Ministers and certifying that any conditions or requirements specified in the certificate—

[^F544]a [^F544]were imposed by a member of the Scottish Executive, and

[^F544]b [^F544]were in operation at a time or throughout a time so specified,

[^F544]shall be conclusive evidence of the matters certified.

[^F544]1B [^F544]In any proceedings under section 17A or 25(8), a certificate signed by or on behalf of the [^F544][[^F548]Welsh Ministers][^F544] and certifying that any conditions or requirements specified in the certificate—

[^F544]a [^F544]were imposed by the [^F544][[^F549]National Assembly for Wales constituted by the Government of Wales Act 1998, the Welsh Ministers, the First Minister for Wales or the Counsel General to the Welsh Assembly Government][^F544], and

[^F544]b [^F544]were in operation at a time or throughout a time so specified,

[^F544]shall be conclusive evidence of the matters certified.]

[^F544]2 [^F544]A document purporting to be such a certificate [^F544][[^F550]as is mentioned in sub-paragraph (1), (1A) or (1B)][^F544] shall be received in evidence and, unless the contrary is proved, be deemed to be such a certificate.

### Part II — Discrimination in Other Areas

#### Restriction on proceedings for breach of Part III

**5** [^F551]1 [^F551]Except as provided by section 25 no civil or criminal proceedings may be brought against any person in respect of an act merely because the act is unlawful under Part III.

#### Period within which proceedings must be brought

**6** [^F552]1 [^F552]A county court or a sheriff court shall not consider a claim under section 25 unless proceedings in respect of the claim are instituted before the end of the period of six months beginning when the act complained of was done.

[^F552][[^F553]2 [^F552]Where, in relation to proceedings or prospective proceedings under section 25, the dispute concerned is referred for conciliation in pursuance of arrangements under section 27 of the Equality Act 2006 before the end of the period of six months mentioned in sub-paragraph (1), the period allowed by that sub-paragraph shall be extended by three months.]

[^F552]3 [^F552]A court may consider any claim under section 25 which is out of time if, in all the circumstances of the case, it considers that it is just and equitable to do so.

[^F552]4 [^F552]For the purposes of sub-paragraph (1)—

[^F552]a [^F552]where an unlawful act of discrimination is attributable to a term in a contract, that act is to be treated as extending throughout the duration of the contract;

[^F552]b [^F552]any act extending over a period shall be treated as done at the end of that period; and

[^F552]c [^F552]a deliberate omission shall be treated as done when the person in question decided upon it.

[^F552]5 [^F552]In the absence of evidence establishing the contrary, a person shall be taken for the purposes of this paragraph to decide upon an omission—

[^F552]a [^F552]when he does an act inconsistent with doing the omitted act; or

[^F552]b [^F552]if he has done no such inconsistent act, when the period expires within which he might reasonably have been expected to do the omitted act if it was to be done.

## [[^F583]SCHEDULE 3A — VALIDITY OF CONTRACTS, COLLECTIVE AGREEMENTS AND RULES OF UNDERTAKINGS

*Section 17C*

### PART I — validity and revision of contracts

**1** (1) A term of a contract is void where –

(a) the making of the contract is, by reason of the inclusion of the term, unlawful by virtue of this Part of this Act;

(b) it is included in furtherance of an act which is unlawful by virtue of this Part of this Act; or

(c) it provides for the doing of an act which is unlawful by virtue of this Part of this Act.

(2) Sub-paragraph (1) does not apply to a term the inclusion of which constitutes, or is in furtherance of, or provides for, unlawful discrimination against, or harassment of, a party to the contract, but the term shall be unenforceable against that party.

(3) A term in a contract which purports to exclude or limit any provision of this Part of this Act is unenforceable by any person in whose favour the term would operate apart from this paragraph.

(4) Sub-paragraphs (1), (2) and (3) apply whether the contract was entered into before or after the date on which this Schedule comes into operation; but in the case of a contract made before that date, those sub-paragraphs do not apply in relation to any period before that date.

**2** (1) Paragraph 1(3) does not apply –

(a) to a contract settling a complaint to which section 17A(1) or 25(8) applies where the contract is made with the assistance of the Agency; or

(b) to a contract settling a complaint to which section 17A(1) or 25(8) applies if the conditions regulating compromise contracts under this Schedule are satisfied in relation to the contract.

(2) The conditions regulating compromise contracts under this Schedule are that –

(a) the contract must be in writing;

(b) the contract must relate to the particular complaint;

(c) the complainant must have received advice from a relevant independent adviser as to the terms and effect of the proposed contract and in particular its effect on his ability to pursue a complaint before an industrial tribunal;

(d) there must be in force, when the adviser gives the advice, a contract of insurance, or an indemnity provided for members of a profession or professional body, covering the risk of a claim by the complainant in respect of loss arising in consequence of the advice;

(e) the contract must identify the adviser; and

(f) the contract must state that the conditions regulating compromise contracts under this Schedule are satisfied.

(3) A person is a relevant independent adviser for the purposes of sub-paragraph (2)(c) –

(a) if he is a qualified lawyer;

(b) if he is an officer, official, employee or member of an independent trade union who has been certified in writing by the trade union as competent to give advice and as authorised to do so on behalf of the trade union; [^F584]. . .

(c) if he works at an advice centre (whether as an employee or a volunteer) and has been certified in writing by the centre as competent to give advice and as authorised to do so on behalf of the centre.[[^F585]; or

(d) if he is a person of a description specified in an order made by the Office.]

(4) But a person is not a relevant independent adviser for the purposes of sub-paragraph (2)(c) in relation to the complainant –

(a) if he is, is employed by or is acting in the matter for the other party or a person who is connected with the other party;

(b) in the case of a person within sub-paragraph (3)(b) or (c), if the trade union or advice centre is the other party or a person who is connected with the other party; or

(c) in the case of a person within sub-paragraph (3)(c), if the complainant makes a payment for the advice received from him.

(5) In sub-paragraph (3)(a) “qualified lawyer” means a barrister (whether in practice as such or employed to give legal advice) or a solicitor who holds a practising certificate.

(6) In sub-paragraph (3)(b) “independent trade union” has the same meaning as in the Industrial Relations (Northern Ireland) Order 1992 [^F586].

(7) For the purposes of sub-paragraph (4)(a) any two persons are to be treated as connected –

(a) if one is a company of which the other (directly or indirectly) has control; or

(b) if both are companies of which a third person (directly or indirectly) has control.

(8) An agreement under which the parties agree to submit a dispute to arbitration –

(a) shall be regarded for the purposes of sub-paragraph (1)(a) and (b) as being a contract settling a complaint if –

(i) the dispute is covered by a scheme having effect by virtue of an order under Article 84A of the Industrial Relations (Northern Ireland) Order 1992 [^F587], and

(ii) the agreement is to submit it to arbitration in accordance with the scheme; but

(b) shall be regarded as neither being nor including such a contract in any other case.

**3** (1) On the application of a disabled person interested in a contract to which paragraph 1(1) or (2) applies, a county court may make such order as it thinks fit for –

(a) removing or modifying any term rendered void by paragraph 1(1), or

(b) removing or modifying any term made unenforceable by paragraph 1(2);

but such an order shall not be made unless all persons affected have been given notice in writing of the application (except where under county court rules notice may be dispensed with) and have been afforded an opportunity to make representations to the court.

(2) An order under sub-paragraph (1) may include provision as respects any period before the making of the order (but after the coming into operation of this Schedule).

### PART II — collective agreements and rules of undertakings

**4** (1) This Part of this Schedule applies to –

(a) any term of a collective agreement, including an agreement which was not intended, or is presumed not to have been intended, to be a legally enforceable contract;

(b) any rule made by an employer for application to all or any of the persons who are employed by him or who apply to be, or are, considered by him for employment;

(c) any rule made by a trade organisation (within the meaning of section 13) or a qualifications body (within the meaning of section 14A) for application to –

(i) all or any of its members or prospective members; or

(ii) all or any of the persons on whom it has conferred authorisations or qualifications or who are seeking the authorisations or qualifications which it has power to confer.

(2) Any term or rule to which this Part of this Schedule applies is void where –

(a) the making of the collective agreement is, by reason of the inclusion of the term, unlawful by virtue of this Part of this Act;

(b) the term or rule is included in furtherance of an act which is unlawful by virtue of this Part of this Act; or

(c) the term or rule provides for the doing of an act which is unlawful by virtue of this Part of this Act.

(3) Sub-paragraph (2) applies whether the agreement was entered into, or the rule made, before or after the date on which this Schedule comes into operation; but in the case of an agreement entered into, or a rule made, before the date on which this Schedule comes into operation, that sub-paragraph does not apply in relation to any period before that date.

**5** A disabled person to whom this paragraph applies may present a complaint to an industrial tribunal that a term or rule is void by virtue of paragraph 4 if he has reason to believe –

(a) that the term or rule may at some future time have effect in relation to him; and

(b) where he alleges that it is void by virtue of paragraph 4(2)(c), that –

(i) an act for the doing of which it provides, may at some such time be done in relation to him, and

(ii) the act would be unlawful by virtue of this Part of this Act if done in relation to him in present circumstances.

**6** In the case of a complaint about –

(a) a term of a collective agreement made by or on behalf of –

(i) an employer,

(ii) an organisation of employers of which an employer is a member, or

(iii) an association of such organisations of one of which an employer is a member, or

(b) a rule made by an employer within the meaning of paragraph 4(1)(b),

paragraph 5 applies to any disabled person who is, or is genuinely and actively seeking to become, one of his employees.

**7** In the case of a complaint about a rule made by an organisation or body to which paragraph 4(1)(c) applies, paragraph 5 applies to any disabled person –

(a) who is, or is genuinely and actively seeking to become, a member of the organisation or body;

(b) on whom the organisation or body has conferred an authorisation or qualification; or

(c) who is genuinely and actively seeking an authorisation or qualification which the organisation or body has power to confer.

**8** (1) When an industrial tribunal finds that a complaint presented to it under paragraph 5 is well-founded the tribunal shall make an order declaring that the term or rule is void.

(2) An order under sub-paragraph (1) may include provision as respects any period before the making of the order (but after the coming into operation of this Schedule).

**9** The avoidance by virtue of paragraph 4(2) of any term or rule which provides for any person to be discriminated against shall be without prejudice to the following rights (except in so far as they enable any person to require another person to be treated less favourably than himself), namely –

(a) such of the rights of the person to be discriminated against, and

(b) such of the rights of any person who will be treated more favourably in direct or indirect consequence of the discrimination,

as are conferred by or in respect of a contract made or modified wholly or partly in pursuance of, or by reference to, that term or rule.

### PART III — interpretation

**10** In this Schedule “collective agreement” means any agreement relating to one or more of the matters mentioned in Article 96(1) of the Industrial Relations (Northern Ireland) Order 1992 (meaning of trade dispute), being an agreement made by or on behalf of one or more employers or one or more organisations of employers or associations of such organisations with one or more organisations of workers or associations of such organisations.

**[[^F588]11** Any reference in this Schedule to this Part of this Act shall be taken to include a reference to Part III of this Act, to the extent that it relates to—

(a) the provision of employment services; or

(b) the provision under a group insurance arrangement of facilities by way of insurance.]

**[[^F589]12** Where a term to which section 26(1A)(c) applies is a term in an agreement which is not a contract, Part I of this Schedule shall have effect as if the agreement were a contract.]]

## SCHEDULE 4 — Premises Occupied Under Leases

*Sections [[^F603]18A(5)] and 27(5) [[^F604]31ADB].*

### Part I — Occupation by [[^F605]EMPLOYER etc]

#### Failure to obtain consent to alteration

**1** [^F606]If any question arises as to whether the occupier has failed to comply with [^F606][[^F607]any duty to make reasonable adjustments][^F606], by failing to make a particular alteration to the premises, any constraint attributable to the fact that he occupies the premises under a lease is to be ignored unless he has applied to the lessor in writing for consent to the making of the alteration.

#### Joining lessors in proceedings under [[^F608]section 17A [^F609]. . . ]

**2** [^F610]1 [^F610]In any proceedings [^F610][[^F611]on a complaint under section 17A][^F610], in a case to which [^F610][[^F612]section 18A][^F610] applies, the complainant or the occupier may ask the tribunal hearing the complaint to direct that the lessor be joined or sisted as a party to the proceedings.

[^F610]2 [^F610]The request shall be granted if it is made before the hearing of the complaint begins.

[^F610]3 [^F610]The tribunal may refuse the request if it is made after the hearing of the complaint begins.

[^F610]4 [^F610]The request may not be granted if it is made after the tribunal has determined the complaint.

[^F610]5 [^F610]Where a lessor has been so joined or sisted as a party to the proceedings, the tribunal may determine—

[^F610]a [^F610]whether the lessor has—

[^F610]i [^F610]refused consent to the alteration, or

[^F610]ii [^F610]consented subject to one or more conditions, and

[^F610]b [^F610]if so, whether the refusal or any of the conditions was unreasonable,

[^F610]6 [^F610]If, under sub-paragraph (5), the tribunal determines that the refusal or any of the conditions was unreasonable it may take one or more of the following steps—

[^F610]a [^F610]make such declaration as it considers appropriate;

[^F610]b [^F610]make an order authorising the occupier to make the alteration specified in the order;

[^F610]c [^F610]order the lessor to pay compensation to the complainant.

[^F610]7 [^F610]An order under sub-paragraph (6)(b) may require the occupier to comply with conditions specified in the order.

[^F610]8 [^F610]Any step taken by the tribunal under sub-paragraph (6) may be in substitution for, or in addition to, any step taken by the tribunal under [^F610][[^F613]section 17A(2)][^F610].

[^F610]9 [^F610]If the tribunal orders the lessor to pay compensation it may not make an order under [^F610][[^F613]section 17A(2)][^F610] ordering the occupier to do so.

#### Regulations

**3** [^F614]Regulations may make provision as to circumstances in which—

[^F614]a [^F614]a lessor is to be taken, for the purposes of [^F614][[^F615]section 18A][^F614] and this Part of this Schedule to have—

[^F614]i [^F614]withheld his consent;

[^F614]ii [^F614]withheld his consent unreasonably;

[^F614]iii [^F614]acted reasonably in withholding his consent;

[^F614]b [^F614]a condition subject to which a lessor has given his consent is to be taken to be reasonable;

[^F614]c [^F614]a condition subject to which a lessor has given his consent is to be taken to be unreasonable.

#### Sub-leases etc.

**4** [^F616]The Secretary of State may by regulations make provision supplementing, or modifying, the provision made by [^F616][[^F617]section 18A][^F616] or any provision made by or under this Part of this Schedule in relation to cases where the occupier occupies premises under a sub-lease or sub-tenancy.

### Part II — Occupation by [[^F618]persons subject to a duty under section 21, 21E or 21H]

#### Failure to obtain consent to alteration

**5** [^F619]If any question arises as to whether the occupier has failed to comply with the section 21 duty [^F619][[^F620]or the duty imposed under section 21E or 21H][^F619], by failing to make a particular alteration to premises, any constraint attributable to the fact that he occupies the premises under a lease is to be ignored unless he has applied to the lessor in writing for consent to the making of the alteration.

#### Reference to court

**6** [^F621]1 [^F621]If the occupier has applied in writing to the lessor for consent to the alteration and—

[^F621]a [^F621]that consent has been refused, or

[^F621]b [^F621]the lessor has made his consent subject to one or more conditions,

[^F621]the occupier or a disabled person who has an interest in the proposed alteration to the premises being made, may refer the matter to a county court or, in Scotland, to the sheriff.

[^F621]2 [^F621]In the following provisions of this Schedule “[^F621]court[^F621]” includes “sheriff”.

[^F621]3 [^F621]On such a reference the court shall determine whether the lessor’s refusal was unreasonable or (as the case may be) whether the condition is, or any of the conditions are, unreasonable.

[^F621]4 [^F621]If the court determines—

[^F621]a [^F621]that the lessor’s refusal was unreasonable, or

[^F621]b [^F621]that the condition is, or any of the conditions are, unreasonable,

[^F621]it may make such declaration as it considers appropriate or an order authorising the occupier to make the alteration specified in the order.

[^F621]5 [^F621]An order under sub-paragraph (4) may require the occupier to comply with conditions specified in the order.

#### Joining lessors in proceedings under section 25

**7** [^F622]1 [^F622]In any proceedings on a claim [^F622][[^F623]under section 25 in a case to which section 27 applies, other than a claim presented as a complaint under section 25(8),][^F622] the plaintiff, the pursuer or the occupier concerned may ask the court to direct that the lessor be joined or sisted as a party to the proceedings.

[^F622]2 [^F622]The request shall be granted if it is made before the hearing of the claim begins.

[^F622]3 [^F622]The court may refuse the request if it is made after the hearing of the claim begins.

[^F622]4 [^F622]The request may not be granted if it is made after the court has determined the claim.

[^F622]5 [^F622]Where a lessor has been so joined or sisted as a party to the proceedings, the court may determine—

[^F622]a [^F622]whether the lessor has—

[^F622]i [^F622]refused consent to the alteration, or

[^F622]ii [^F622]consented subject to one or more conditions, and

[^F622]b [^F622]if so, whether the refusal or any of the conditions was unreasonable.

[^F622]6 [^F622]If, under sub-paragraph (5), the court determines that the refusal or any of the conditions was unreasonable it may take one or more of the following steps—

[^F622]a [^F622]make such declaration as it considers appropriate;

[^F622]b [^F622]make an order authorising the occupier to make the alteration specified in the order;

[^F622]c [^F622]order the lessor to pay compensation to the complainant.

[^F622]7 [^F622]An order under sub-paragraph (6)(b) may require the occupier to comply with conditions specified in the order.

[^F622]8 [^F622]If the court orders the lessor to pay compensation it may not order the occupier to do so.

#### [^F624][[^F625]Joining lessors in proceedings relating to group insurance or employment services]

**[[^F625]7A** [^F626]. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .]

#### Regulations

**8** [^F627]Regulations may make provision as to circumstances in which—

[^F627]a [^F627]a lessor is to be taken, for the purposes of section 27 and this Part of this Schedule to have—

[^F627]i [^F627]withheld his consent;

[^F627]ii [^F627]withheld his consent unreasonably;

[^F627]iii [^F627]acted reasonably in withholding his consent;

[^F627]b [^F627]a condition subject to which a lessor has given his consent is to be taken to be reasonable;

[^F627]c [^F627]a condition subject to which a lessor has given his consent is to be taken to be unreasonable.

#### Sub-leases etc.

**9** [^F628]The Secretary of State may by regulations make provision supplementing, or modifying, the provision made by section 27 or any provision made by or under this Part of this Schedule in relation to cases where the occupier occupies premises under a sub-lease or sub-tenancy.

**1** [^F703]In its application to Northern Ireland this Act shall have effect subject to the following modifications.

**2** [^F704]1 [^F704][^F705]. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .

[^F704]2 [^F704]In section 3 for subsections (4) to (12) substitute—

> [^F704]4 [^F704]In preparing a draft of any guidance, the Department shall consult such persons as it considers appropriate.
>
> [^F704]5 [^F704]Where the Department proposes to issue any guidance, the Department shall publish a draft of it, consider any representations that are made to the Department about the draft and, if the Department thinks it appropriate, modify its proposals in the light of any of those representations.
>
> [^F704]6 [^F704]If the Department decides to proceed with any proposed guidance, the Department shall lay a draft of it before the Assembly.
>
> [^F704]7 [^F704]If, within the statutory period, the Assembly resolves not to approve the draft, the Department shall take no further steps in relation to the proposed guidance.
>
> [^F704]8 [^F704]If no such resolution is made within the statutory period, the Department shall issue the guidance in the form of its draft.
>
> [^F704]9 [^F704]The guidance shall come into force on such date as the Department may by order appoint.
>
> [^F704]10 [^F704]Subsection (7) does not prevent a new draft of the proposed guidance being laid before the Assembly.
>
> [^F704]11 [^F704]The Department may—
>
> [^F704]a [^F704]from time to time revise the whole or any part of any guidance and re-issue it ;
>
> [^F704]b [^F704]by order revoke any guidance.
>
> [^F704]12 [^F704]In this section—
>
> - [^F704]“[^F704]the Department[^F704]” means the Department of Economic Development;
> - [^F704]“[^F704]guidance[^F704]” means guidance issued by the Department under this section and includes guidance which has been revised and re-issued;
> - [^F704]“[^F704]statutory period[^F704]” has the meaning assigned to it by section 41(2) of the Interpretation Act (Northern Ireland) 1954.

**[^F752]35** [^F753]For section 52 substitute—

> **[^F753] Further provisions about codes issued under section 51.**
>
> **[^F753]52** [^F753]1 [^F753]In this section—
>
> - [^F753]“[^F753]proposal[^F753]” means a proposal made by [^F753][[^F754]the Equality Commission for Northern Ireland][^F753] to a Northern Ireland department under section 51;
>
> - [^F753]“[^F753]responsible department[^F753]”—
>
>   - (a) [^F753]in relation to a proposal, means the Northern Ireland department to which the proposal is made,
>   - (b) [^F753]in relation to a code, means the Northern Ireland department by which the code is issued; and
>
> - [^F753]“[^F753]statutory period[^F753]” has the meaning assigned to it by section 41(2) of the Interpretation Act (Northern Ireland) 1954.
>
> [^F753]2 [^F753]In preparing any proposal, [^F753][[^F754]the Equality Commission for Northern Ireland][^F753] shall consult—
>
> [^F753]a [^F753]such persons (if any) as the responsible department has specified in making its request to [^F753][[^F754]the Equality Commission for Northern Ireland][^F753]; and
>
> [^F753]b [^F753]such other persons (if any) as [^F753][[^F754]the Equality Commission for Northern Ireland][^F753] considers appropriate.
>
> [^F753]3 [^F753]Before making any proposal [^F753][[^F754]the Equality Commission for Northern Ireland][^F753] shall publish a draft, consider any representations made to it about the draft and, if it thinks it appropriate, modify its proposal in the light of any of those representations.
>
> [^F753]4 [^F753]Where [^F753][[^F754]the Equality Commission for Northern Ireland][^F753] makes any proposal, the responsible department may—
>
> [^F753]a [^F753]approve it;
>
> [^F753]b [^F753]approve it subject to such modifications as that department thinks appropriate; or
>
> [^F753]c [^F753]refuse to approve it.
>
> [^F753]5 [^F753]Where the responsible department approves any proposal (with or without modifications) that department shall prepare a draft of the proposed code and lay it before the Assembly.
>
> [^F753]6 [^F753]If, within the statutory period, the Assembly resolves not to approve the draft, the responsible department shall take no further steps in relation to the proposed code.
>
> [^F753]7 [^F753]If no such resolution is made within the statutory period, the responsible department shall issue the code in the form of its draft.
>
> [^F753]8 [^F753]The code shall come into force on such date as the responsible department may appoint by order.
>
> [^F753]9 [^F753]Subsection (6) does not prevent a new draft of the proposed code from being laid before the Assembly.
>
> [^F753]10 [^F753]If the responsible department refuses to approve a proposal, that department shall give [^F753][[^F754]the Equality Commission for Northern Ireland][^F753] a written statement of the department’s reasons for not approving it.
>
> [^F753]11 [^F753]The responsible department may by order revoke a code.

[^F753].

> **[^F756] Further provisions about codes issued under section 53.**
>
> **[^F756]54** [^F756]1 [^F756]In preparing a draft of any code under section 53, the Department shall consult such organisations representing the interests of employers or of disabled persons in, or seeking, employment as the Department considers appropriate.

> [^F756]3 [^F756]If the Department decides to proceed with the code, the Department shall lay a draft of it before the Assembly.
>
> [^F756]4 [^F756]If, within the statutory period, the Assembly resolves not to approve the draft, the Department shall take no further steps in relation to the proposed code.
>
> [^F756]5 [^F756]If no such resolution is made within the statutory period, the Department shall issue the code in the form of its draft.
>
> [^F756]6 [^F756]The code shall come into force on such date as the Department may appoint by order.
>
> [^F756]7 [^F756]Subsection (4) does not prevent a new draft of the proposed code from being laid before the Assembly.
>
> [^F756]8 [^F756]The Department may by order revoke a code.
>
> [^F756]9 [^F756]In this section—
>
> - [^F756]“[^F756]the Department[^F756]” means the Department of Economic Development; and
> - [^F756]“[^F756]statutory period[^F756]” has the meaning assigned to it by section 41(2) of the Interpretation Act (Northern Ireland) 1954.
